504. (1) Save as expressly provided in this Act and unless the context otherwise requires,—

(a) any reference in this Act to the Commissioner and the Standing Committee shall be construed—

(i) in relation to any matter pertaining to the Delhi Electric Supply Undertaking, as a reference respectively to the General Manager (Electricity) and the Delhi Electric Supply Committee;

(ii) In relation to any matter pertaining to the Delhi Transport Undertaking, as a reference respectively to the General Manager (Transport) and the Delhi Transport Committee; and

(b) any reference in this Act to the Standing Committee shall be construed in relation to any matter pertaining to the x^ Delhi Water Supply and Sewage Disposal Undertaking, as a reference to the Delhi Water Supply and Sewage Disposal Commitee.

(2) After the establishment of the Corporation any reference in any enactment, rule, bye-law, order, scheme, notification or other 2 0 instrument having the force of law, to any of the bodies or local authorities specified in the Second Schedule shall, unless the context or subject otherwise requires, be construed as a reference to the Corporation.

507. Notwithstanding anything contained in the foregoing provisions of this Act,—

Special provisions as to rural areas.

(a) the Corporation with the previous approval of the Central Government, may, by notification in the Official Gazette, declare that any porcion of the rural areas shall cease to be included therein and upon the issue of such notification that portion shall be included in and form part of the urban areas;

(5) the Corporation with the previous approval of the Central Government may, by notification in the Official Gazette,—

(i) exempt the rural areas or any portion thereof from such of the provi3ions of this Act as it deems fit,

(ii) levy taxes, rates, fees and other charges in the rural areas or any portion thereof at rates lower than those at which such laxes, rates, fees and other charges are levied in the urban areas or exempt such areas or portion from any such tax, rate, fee or other charge;

(c) the Corporation shall pay to a Gaon Sabha an amount equal to the proceeds of—

(i) the tax on professions, trades, callings and employments, as and when thai tax is levied in the Gaon Sabha area; and

(ii) the general tax on lands and buildings in that area, ^ after deducting the cosL of collection from such proceeds.

Spe ial provisions as to Red Fort Area.

508. (1) In adm'nister'ng the prov.sions of this Act within the Red Fort Area the Corporation shall comply with the military rules and regulations for the time being in force within that area.

(2) In this section the expression "Red Fort Area" means the 3° Red Fort and such areas contiguous thereto as may be declared by the Central Government by notification in the Gazette of India to be included within the Red Fort Area.

CHAPTER XXVI SUPPLEMENTAL AND TRANSITIONAL PROVISIONS Power of Central Government to make first appointments.

509. (1) Notwithstanding anything contained in section 59 or in section 88 or section 91, the first appointment to the post of the General Manager (Electricity) or the General Manager (Transport) or the first appointment to the post of any municipal officer or other 75§

THE 6A2ETTE Of iNblA E3WRAOR£>INARY \PABX Ii municipal employee may be made by the Central Government so however that the term of any such appointment shall not in any case exceed three years:

Provided that the power under this sub-section shall not be exercised after the expiry of three years from the commencement 5 of this Act.

(2) Notwithstanding anything contained in section 95, in making any appointment of any municipal officer or other municipal employee under sub-section (1) it shall not be necessary for the Central Government to consult the Union Public Service Commission. io

(3) Every person appointed under sub-section (1) shall receive such salary and allowances as the Central Government may determine.

Provisions as to employees of bodies and local authorities

510. (1) Every officer and other employee of each of the bodies and local authorities specified in the Second Schedule shall, on and 15 from the establishment of the Corporation, be transferred to and become an officer or other employee of the Corporation with whoso functions are taken over by the Corporation. such designation as the Commissioner may determine and shall hold office by the same tenure, at the same remuneration and on the same terms and conditions of service as he would have held the same if 20 the Corporation had not been established, and shall continue to do so unless and until such tenure, remuneration and terms and conditions are duly altered by the Corporation:

Provided that any service rendered by any such officer or other employee before the establishment of the Corporation shall be 25 deemed to be service rendered under the Corporation.

(2) The Commissioner may employ any officer or other employee transferred to the Corporation under sub-section (1) in the discharge of such functions under this Act as the Commissioner may think proper and every such officer or other employee shall discharge 30 those functions accordingly.

Special provisions as to the area transferred from New Delhi to Delhi.

511. (I) In this section "transferred area" means that area of Delhi which immediately before the commencement of this Act is included within the local limits of the New Delhi Municipal Committee but as from such commencement is included in and forms 35 part of Delhi by virtue of the provisions of this Act.

(2) As from the establishment of the Corporation,—

(a) all lands and buildings (together with all interests of whatsoever nature and kinds therein) situated in the transferred area and vested in the New Delhi Municipal Committee 40 7 eg immediately before such establishment shall pass to and vest In the Corporation;

(b) all stores, articles and other movable properties belonging to the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment and utilised for or In connection with the transferred area shall pass to and vest in the Corporation;

(c) all appointments, notifications, orders, schemes, rules, forms, notices or bye-laws made or issued or any licence or permission granted by the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment in or in connection with the transferred area shall, in so far as they are not inconsistent with the provisions of this Act continue in force and be deemed to have been made, issued or granted under the provisions of this Act unless and until they are superseded by any appointment, !5 notification, order, scheme, rule, form, notice or bye-law made or issued or any licence or permission granted under the said provisions;

(d) all debts, obligations and liabilities incurred, all contracts entered into and all matters and things engaged to be done by, with or for the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment for or in connection with the transferred area shall be deemed to have been incurred, entered into, engaged to be done by, with or for the Corporation or the municipal authority concerned;

(e) all assessments, valuations, measurements or divisions made by the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment in or in connection with the transferred area shall in so far as they are not inconsistent with the provisions of this Act, continue in force and be deemed to have g0 been made under the provisions of this Act unless and until they are superseded by any assessment, valuation, measurement or division made by the Corporation or the municipal authority concerned under the said provisions;

(f) all rates, taxes, fees, rents and other sums of money ^ due to the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment shall be deemed to be due to the Corporation;

(g) all rates, taxes, fees, rents and other charges shall unless and until they are varied by the Corporation, continue to be levied at the same rate at which they were being levied by the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such 4° establishment; 7<3o

(h) all suits, prosecutions and other legal proceedings instituted or which might have been instituted by or against the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment for any matter in relation to the transferred area may be continued or instituted by or against the Corporation;

(i) every officer and other employee serving under the New Delhi Municipal Committee immediately before such establishment in connection with the transferred area shall be transferred to and become an officer or other employee of the Corporation with such designation as the Commissioner may 10 determine and hold office by the same tenure and at the same remuneration and on the same terms and conditions of service as he wouid have held the same if the Corporation had not been establ shed and shall continue to do so unless and until such tenure, remuneration and terms and conditions are duly altered T5 by the Corporation:

Provided that any service rendered by any such officer or other employee before the establishment of the Corporation shall be deemed to be service rendered under the Corporation:

Provided further that the Commissioner may employ any such 20 officer or other employee in the discharge of such functions under this Act as the Commissioner may think proper and every such officer or other employee shall discharge those funct.ons accordingly.

(3) As soon as may be after the commencement of this Act, the Central Government shall decide—

(a) which stores, articles and other movable properties referred to in clause (b) of sub-section (2) have been utilised by the New Delhi Municipal Committee i'or or in connection with the transferred area;

(b) which debts, obligations, liabilities, contracts, matters 3 O and things referred to in clause (d) of the said sub-section have been incurred, entered into or engaged to be done by, with or for the New Delhi Municipal Committee for or in connection with the transferred area;

(c) which officers and other employees referred to in clause - ,

(i)of that sub-section served under the said Committee in connection with the transferred area.

(4) In case of any dispute in relation to any of the matters referred to in the various clauses of sub-section (2) other than clauses (b), (d) and (i), the dispute may be referred to the Centra] Government by the Corporation or the New Delhi Municipal Com mlttee and the decision of that Government shall be final. <Urc. 2]

512. (1) Any expenditure incurred dur'ng the period between the commencement of this Act and the establishment of the Corporation under any of the provisions of this Act shall be borne by thp Central Government and the amoun', of the expenditure so in5 curred shall on fuch establishment be recoverable by that Government out of the Municipal Fund.

Expenditure in connection with the municipal affairs from the commencement of this Act to the adoption of the budget by the Corporation,

(2) If In respect of the period between the establishment of 1ht Corporation and the adoption of the first budget by the Corporation further expenditure from Ihe Municipal Fund becomes necessary 10 over and above the expenditure theretofor authorised for that year by the sanctioned budget estimates of the various bodies and local authorises specified in the Second Schedule, the Corporation shall adopt a supplementary statement showing the estimated amount of that expenditure.

T5

(3) Every item of expenditure shown in the supplementary statement as adopted by the Corporation shall be deemed to be expenditure covered by a current budget-grant within the meaning of section 101.

(4) The supplementary statement shall be prepared, presented 2 0 and adopted in such manner and shall provide for such matters as may be determined by the Corporation.

Limits of New Delhi Municipality-

513. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Punjab Municipal Act, 1911, as applicable to New Delhi Municipality or in any notification issued thereunder, the limits of that Municipality shall be as 25 described in the First Schedule.

Power to remove difficulties

514. If any difficulty arises in relat'on to the transition from the provisions of any of the enactments referred to in sect'on 515, or In giving effect to the provisions of this Ac\ the Central Government may, by order as occasion requires, do anything which appears to 30 it to be necessary for the purpose of removing the difficulty:

Provided that no such order shall be made after the expiration of two years from the establishment of the Corporation.

Repettl, etc. and saving*

515. (1) As from the date of the establishment of the Corporation,— '3 °f 1926. 13 of 1950.

(a) the Delhi Joint Water and Sewage Board Act, 1926, and the Delhi Road Transport Authority Act, 1950, shall stand repealed; and

(b) the enactments specified in the Thirteenth Schedule shall cease to have effect within Delhi,

(2) Nothwithstandlng the provisions of sub-section (1) of this section or of clause (a) of sub-section (2) of section 285,—

(a) any appointment, notification, order, scheme, rule, form, notice or bye-law made or issued, and any licence or permission granted under any of the Acts or enactments referred to in that 5 sub-section and in force immediately before the establishment of the Corporation, shall, in so far as it is not inconsistent with the provisions of this Act, continue in force and be deemed to have been made, issued or granted under the provisions of this Act, unless and' until it is superseded by any appointment, IO notification, order, scheme, rule, form, notice or bye-law made or issued or any licence or permission granted under the said provisions;

(b) all debts, obligations and liabilities incurred, all contracts entered into and all matters and things engaged to be l$ done by, with or for any of the bodies or local authorities specified in the Second Schedule before such establishment shall be deemed to have been incurred, entered into or engaged to be done by, with or for the Corporation or the municipal authority concerned;

(c) all budget estimates, assessments, valuations, measurements or divisions made by any of the aforesaid bodies or local authorities shall in so far as they are not inconsistent with the provisions of this Act, continue in force and be deemed to have been made under the provisions of this Act unless and until 2^ they are superseded by any budget estimate, assessment, valuation, measurement or division made by the Corporation or the municipal authority concerned under the said provisions;

(d) all properties, movable and immovable and all interests of whatsoever nature and kinds therein, vested in any of the 30 aforesaid bodies and local authorities immediately before such establishment shall, with all rights of whatsoever description, used, enjoyed or possessed by any such body or authority shall vest in the Corporation;

(e) all rates, taxes, fees, rents and other sums of money due 35 to any of the aforesaid bodies or local authorities immediately before such establishment shall be deemed to be due to the Corporation;

(f) all rates, taxes, fees, rents, fares and other charges shall until and unless they are varied by the Corporation continue to 4° be levied at the same rate at which they were being levied by Sic. 2] the aforesaid bodies or local authorities immediately before such establishment;

(g) all suits, prosecutions and other legal proceedings instituted or which might have been instituted by or against any of the aforesaid bodies or local authorities may be continued or instituted by or against the Corporation. 643 Q o* 1—32

THE THIRTEENTH

SCHEDULE (See section 515) ENACTMENTS CEASING TO HAVE EFFECT IN DELHI n

1. The Punjab Municipal Act, 1911, as in force in Delhi immediately before the commencement of this Act,

2. The provisions of the City of Bombay Corporation Act, 1888, as in force in Delhi immediately before the commencement of this Act. 3- The provisions of the United Provinces Municipalities Act, 1916, as in force in Delhi immediately before the commencement of this Act.

4. The Punjab District Boards Act, 1883, as in force in Delhi immediately before the commencement of thie A c

[PART it STATEMENT OF OBJECTS AND REASONS The question of establishing a Municipal Corporation in Delhi has been under the consideration of the Government of India for some time. On the appointment of the States Reorganisation Commission, it was decided that the matter should be deferred until the Commission had reported. The States Reorganisation Commission, while recommending that municipal autonomy in the form of a Corporation was i(the right and in fact the only solution of the problem of Delhi State", observed that in the event of this recommendation being accepted, it was for the Government of India to determine whether the Municipal set-up of Delhi should follow a two-tier model on the lines of the London County Council or whether there should be one or two Corporations of the pattern already under the consideration of the Government of India.

2. On September 4, 1956, the Minister for Home Affairs stated in the Lok Sabha that a Corporation on the lines of the Bombay Municipal Corporation would be established in Delhi. He also outlined the broad features of the scheme for the proposed Corporation. The Bill seeks to provide for the establishment of a Municipal Corporation in Delhi in accordance with the statement made by the Home Minister.

3. The main features of the scheme are: —

(i) extension of the jurisdiction of the proposed Corporation to the entire territory of Delhi excluding only some parts of New Delhi and the Cantonment area,

(ii) election of 80 Councillors on the basis of adult suffrage, of whom 12 will be members of Scheduled Castes,

(iil) transfer to the Corporation of the functions of the existing Water and Sewage. Transport and Electricity Boards,

(iv) constitution of statutory committees to deal with

(a) electricity supply, (b) transport, (c) supply of water and disposal of sewage, and (d) rural areas, and

(v) conversion of the Corporation together with an appropriate number of members to be elected from the excluded areas of New Delhi and Delhi Cantonment into an electoral college for election of the representatives of this Union territory to Rajya Sabha. 79c) The functions proposed to ba entrusted to the Corporation follow those vesting in the Bombay Municipal Corporation except for some pdditions to the discretionary functions of the Corporation so as to enlarge the scope of its social welfare activities. Provision has also been made for consulting the U.P.S.C. as respects the higher services of the Corporation.

4. The Bill makes the necessary supplemental and transitional provisions for the making of first appointments, absorption of employees of the merging local bodies and boards, vesting of property and apportionment of assets and liabilities, and for the expenditure to be incurred during the period between the commencement of the Act and the establishment of the Corporation.

NEW DELHI;

The 2nd September. 1957.

GOVIND BALLABH PAN! 8oo

THE GAZETTE OP INDfA EXTRAORDINARY Notes on clauses Clause 2, sub-clause (11).—The jurisdiction of the Corporation will extend to the whole of the Union territory of Delhi excluding the part of present New Delhi specified in the First Schedule, and the area of the Delhi Cantonment.

Clause 3.—The Corporation will be established as soon as the first general election, held on the basis of adult suffrage, is over. It Will consist of eighty councillors. Twelve seats are proposed to be reserved for the Scheduled Castes, on the basis of their population.

Clause 4.—The term of office of the councillors is proposed to be fixed at four years commencing from the date of publication of the result of election under clause 13 but, in the case of the initial constitution of the Corporation, the term will commence from the date of issue of the notification under clause 3(1).

Clauses 5 and 6.—It is proposed that the delimitation of wards should be made by an order of the Central Government but any alteration or amendment in it will be made by the Corporation with the previous approval of that Government.

Clause 7.—As it is proposed to hold elections to the Corporation en the basis of adult suffrage, the rolls prepared for elections to the Lok Sabha can be utilised for elections to the Corporation, and hence provision has not been made for the preparation of separate rolls for the Corporation except by way of splitting the parliamentary electoral rolls.

Clauses 8 and 9.—These clauses prescribe the qualifications and disqualifications for election as councillor.

Clause 10.—While it has been provided that every elector shall have one vote; in the case of plural-member wards every elector shall have one vote for each councillor to be elected.

Clauses 14 to 20.—These clauses "deal with disputes relating to elections. It has been provided that an election shall be called in question only by means of an election petition to be presented to the court of the district judge of Delhi. A petitioner may claim that the election of all or any of the returned candidates be declared void and that he himself or any other candidate may be declared to have been duly elected. The decision of the district judge on an election petition shall be final and conclusive.

Clause 21.—This clause prescribes the corrupt practices relating to elections, generally on the lines of the provisions contained in section 123 of the Representation of the People Act, 1951.

SEC; 2] 8ol Clause 32.—The question whether a councillor has become subject to any of the disqualifications mentioned in clause 9, will be referred to the district judge, whose decision is proposed to be made final.

Clause 33.—Provision has been made for payment of allowances to councillors for attendance at meetings of the Corporation and iis committees.

Clause 35,—This clause deals with the allowances and housing and other facilities that may be given to the Mayor and also empowers him to have full access to all the records of the Corporation.

Clause 38.—It is proposed to appoint a Rural Areas Committee to advise the Corporation in relation to its functions in the rural areas, including the imposition of taxes. The Committee will consist of all the councillors elected from wards situated within the rural areas.

Clauses 41 and 42.—These enumerate the obligatory and discretionary functions of the Corporation.

Clause 43.—The statutory authorities of the Corporation have been enumerated in this clause. They will ensure efficient end speedy transaction of business within their respective spheres of work. Their constitution and functions have been enumerated in clauses 44 to 70.

The provision for nomination of three members of the Delhi Eleefric Supply Committee, the Delhi Transport Committee and the Delhi Water Supply and Sewage Disposal Committee has been made so that the presence of persons having experience of administration and industrial, commercial, financial or labour matters and having knowledge and experience of matters connected with the Undertakings, may strengthen the Committees.

Clause 69(1).—This clause provides that the Corporation may call for any record for inspection.

Clause 78.—This clause deals with the maintsnance of order at meetings of the Corporation and authorises the Mayor to suspend a member in certain circumstances.

Clause 95.—Provision has been made to the effect that for appointments to any posts carrying a minimum monthly salary of Rs. 350 or more (exclusive of allowance), consultation with the Union Public Service Commission shall be necessary, except to the extent provided in the clause.

Clause 112.—This clause deals with the levy of taxes by the Corporation and detailed provisions in the matter are contained in the other clauses of Chapter VIII.

[PABT II Clause 177.—This clause, read with the Tenth Schedule, provides for the levy of terminal tax on goods at the rates at which the tax is being levied at present within the area of the Delhi Municipal Committee. This clause empowers the Corporation to vary the rates of the terminal tax but when such rates are Increased, the increased rates shall not be more than double the present rates.

Clause 179.—The proceeds of the terminal tax, reduced by the cost of collection, will be payable, under this clause, to the Corporation.

Clause 183.—The proceeds of the tax on motor vehicles and the proceeds of the entertainment and the betting taxes collected within the jurisdiction of the Corporation are, under this clause, payable to the Corporation after deducting the cost of collection.

Clause 184.—This clause deals with the borrowing powers of the Corporation, It will not be permissible to raise any loan without the previous sanction of the Central Government.

Clause 204.—This clause provides for a monthly examination and audit of municipal accounts. The powers and duties of the Municipal Chief Auditor have been detailed in clause 207.

Clause 274.—This clause provides that the Delhi Electric Supply Committee shall be responsible for the development and maintenance of an efficient, co-ordinated and economical system of electric supply for the whole of the Union territory of Delhi.

Clause 287.—This clause provides that the Delhi Transport Committee shall be responsible for maintenance of an efficient, adequate, economical and properly co-ordinated system of road transport services.

Clause 331.—It is proposed that for the erection of, and addition and alteration in, buildings the sanction of the Corporation should be obtained.

Clause 364,—This clause deals with the removal of congested buildings.

Clause 425.—This and the subsequent four clauses deal with the schemes relating to city improvement and re-housing.

Clause 461.—Read with the Twelfth Schedule, this clause prescribes the punishment for offences under the Act.

Clause 505.—Provision has been made for the functioning of Gaon Panchayats alongside with the Corporation and for this purpose the necessary re-adjustment of functions has been made to fit them suitably in the scheme of the Municipal Corporation. A re-adjustment has also been made in regard to the levy of taxes by the Gaon Panchayats.

Sic. 2] Clause 506.—The eighty councillors of the Delhi Municipal Corporation, along with ten persons to be chosen by direct election on the basis of adult suffrage from the areas within the New Delhi Municipal Committee and the Delhi Cantonment Board, will form the electoral college for election to the Rajya Sabha from the Union territory.

Clause 507.—This clause empowers the Corporation, with the previous approval of the Central Government, to declare that any area included in the rural areas shall cease to be so included or that the rural areas or any portion thereof shall be exempt from any of the provisions of the Act as also from the levy of any taxes, rates, fees or other charges. It also provides for the levy of the taxes and rateB, etc., at lower rates.

Clause 509.—This clause deals with the Central Government's power to make first appointments for a limited period of three years from the commencement of the Act.

Clause 510.—This clause makes provision regarding the continuance in service of employees of the various bodies and local authorities specified in the Second Schedule, and for the continuance jf their terms and conditions of service.

Clause 511.—This clause deals with the distribution of assets and liabilities relating to the area to be transferred from New Delhi, to the Corporation.

Clause 512.—Any expenditure incurred during the period between the commencement of the Act and the establishment of the Corporation under any of the provisions of the Act will be borne by the Central Government but will be recoverable out of the Municipal Fund.

Clause 514.—As contingencies not envisaged in the Bill may arise, this clause deals with the power to remove difficulties.

Clause 515.—This clause provides that the enactments mentioned in the Thirteenth Schedule shall cease to have effect within the jurisdiction of the Corporation from the date on which the Corporation is established. The provisions of sections 159, 160 and 165 of the Punjab Municipal Act, 1911, which deal with certain customary rights in respect of house scavenging will, therefore, cease to be operative.

[PAST II FINANCIAL MEMORANDUM Clause 179 of the Bill provides that the proceeds of the terminal tax shall be paid by the Central Government to the Corporation, and to other local authorities within the Union territory of Delhi, in such proportion as may be determined by that Government from time to time. Similarly, clause 183 of the Bill provides that th« proceeds of the tax on motor vehicles collected under the Punjab Motor Vehicles Taxation Act, 1924, as extended to -Delhi, and of the entertainment and betting taxes collected under the provisions oi the U.P.- Entertainment and Betting Tax Act, 1937, as extended to Delhi, shall be paid to the Corporation for the performance of its functions.

While the proceeds from these three taxes will form part of the Consolidated Fund of India, when Parliament by appropriation made by law in this behalf so provides, they will be payable to the Corporation after deducting the cost of collection. ' 2. There is no uniformity regarding the rates of terminal tax at present levied in the various local bodies in Delhi, and provision has therefore been made in clause 177 (read with the Tenth Schedule) for their levy and collection within the entire area of the Union territory at the rates in force within the area of the Delhi Municipal. Committee. It is expected that the payments to the Delhi Municipal Corporation on account of the terminal tax, the motor vehicles tax and the entertainment and betting taxes will amount approximately to Rs. 108 lakhs, Rs. 12 lakhs and Rs. 30 lakhs, respectively.

The provision for payment on account of the entertainment and betting taxes is based on the recommendation of the Taxation Enquiry Commission. 3- According to sub-clause (r) of clause 41, the establishment, maintenance of and aid to schools for primary education will be one of the obligatory functions of the Corporation. This will be subject to such grants as may be determined by the Central Government from time to time. At the rate at which such grants are bemg paid to those local bodies which will merge in the Corporation, a sum of approximately Rs. 26 lakhs will be payable to the Corporation. It is however proposed to appoint a special officer to examine the financial resources of the Corporation so as to determine the grants to be paid by the Central Government.

4. Provision has been made in clause 512 that any expenditure Incurred during the period between the commencement of the Act and the establishment of the Corporation under any of the provisions of the Act shall be borne by the Central Government and recovered from the Municipal Fund on the establishment of the Corporation BEfc. 2] MEMORANDUM REGARDING DELEGATED LEGISLATION Clauses 21(6), 30, 33, 49(8), 182, 259(3) and 265(2) empower the Central Government to make rules.

Sub-clause (6) of clause authorises the Central Government to prescribe any practice, in addition to those specified in sub-clauses (1) to (5), to be a corrupt practice for purposes of election. Clause 30 provides for the making of rules relating to the electoral rolls, appointment of election officers, nomination of candidates, scrutiny of nominations, casting and counting of votes, settlement of election disputes and other matters connected with the election of councillors.

Clauses 33 and 49(3) relate to the rates at which allowances are to be paid to the councillors and the nominated members of the Delhi Electric Supply Committee, the Delhi Transport Committee and the Delhi Water Supply and Sewage Disposal Committee for attendance at meetings, while clause 35 provides for the making of rules relating to the allowance payable to the Mayor.

Clause 182 relates io the rules to be made for the levy, assessment and collection of terminal tax.

Clause 259(3) authorises the Central Government to determine the items of, expenditure to be taken into account for calculating the final isiue rate of water supplied to the New Delhi Municipal Committee and the Military Engineer, Delhi Cantonment and clause 265(2) makes a similar provision in regard to the total net cost of the disposal of sewage.

The Corporation has been given the power to make regulations in respect of matters like service conditions of employees (clauses 58 and 62), procedure and transaction of business at meetings of the Corporation and at meetings of its committees [clauses 77(2), 78(5), 81 and 83], constitution of special funds (clause 107), form in which budget estimates [clause 108(3)], borrowing by Corporation (clause 195), maintenance of accounts (clause 203).

Clause 480 provides that all such regulations shall be subject to the approval of the Central Government.

The Corporation has been given the power under clause 481 to make bye-laws relating to taxation, water supply, drainage and sewage disposal, electric supply, transport services, streets, build* ing9, sanitation and public health, vital statistics, public safety and suppression of nuisances, markets, slaughter houses, trades and occupations, city improvement and other miscellaneous matter? relating to the municipal government of Delhi. 8o6 The various matters referred to above are matters of detail and are necessary for the day-to-day administration of the municipal affairs of Delhi. It is neither desirable nor practicable to put all these matters in the Bill which is itself a voluminous one. The delegated legislation is of the normal type.

M. N. KAUL, Secretary.
